Now, Shri K.R.N. Rajeshkumar. 
Demand for tax exemption and enhanced support for National Bank for Agriculture 
and Rural Development (NABARD) 
SHRI K.R.N. RAJESHKUMAR (Tamil Nadu): Vanakkam, hon. Chairman, Sir. I rise 
today to draw the attention of this House and the Government to the critical role of the 
National Bank for Agriculture and Rural Development (NABARD) and the urgent need 
to strengthen its financial autonomy and operational capacity. The NABARD is the 
backbone of our rural economy. It plays an important role in agricultural credit, rural 
infrastructure development, and helps in maintaining incomes across the nation. It is 
the true Mother Bird of our rural sector development and in supporting the very 
foundation of our nation's wealth. However, the financial framework, under which the 
NABARD operates, severely restricts its ability to fulfil its mandate. The imposition of 
taxes on NABARD's operations drains resources that should directly uplift rural 
communities. I urge the Government to remove Income Tax imposed on NABARD.  
Additionally, the allocation for the Rural Infrastructure Development Fund (RIDF), 
managed by the NABARD, must be substantially increased. The RIDF is crucial for 
rural infrastructure, market access and irrigation, yet its current allocation is 
inadequate. Moreover, the RIDF resources must be exclusively directed towards 
[ 10 February,2026, ]
25

agricultural and rural development. While MSMEs, NHB and SIDBI are important, 
their funding should not come at NABARD's expense. The NABARD needs higher 
budgetary support, greater autonomy and investments in technology and human 
resources to fulfil its mission effectively. Sir, I urge the Government to remove 
NABARD's income tax burden, increase the RIDF allocation and empower NABARD 
to drive rural wealth. Strengthening NABARD means strengthening our nation. Thank 
you, Sir.                                                                                  
MR. CHAIRMAN: The following hon. Members associated themselves with the matter 
raised by the hon. Member, Shri K.R.N. Rajeshkumar:  Shri P. Wilson (Tamil Nadu),

(Kerala), Shri A.A. Rahim (Kerala), Shri Haris Beeran (Kerala), Shrimati Sagarika 
Ghose (West Bengal), Dr. John Brittas (Kerala), Dr. Fauzia Khan (Maharashtra), 
Shri Niranjan Bishi (Odisha), Shri R. Girirajan (Tamil Nadu) and Shrimati Rajathi 
(Tamil Nadu).

Need for institutionalising an Annual Report on the state of social justice 
SHRI MUKUL BALKRISHNA WASNIK (Rajasthan): Mr. Chairman, Sir, I wish to draw 
the attention of the Government to an important matter and hope that the 
Government takes it seriously. Just as the Economic Survey is laid on the Table of the 
House one day prior to the Union Budget, India should institutionalize an Annual 
Report on the state of social justice that will be similarly laid on the Table of the 
House. This Report should serve as a data-driven strategic guide to uphold the 
constitutional values of equality and inclusivity and should be available for discussion 
in and outside the Parliament. This Report on the state of social justice must 
systematically evaluate existing legal and policy frameworks to ensure they meet 
constitutional mandates. Essential areas of focus must include number one, security 
- examining the implementation of various schemes and laws that exist for the 
protection of the life and dignity of persons from the disadvantaged sections. The 
growing number of atrocities against SCs, STs, women and the rise in cases of 
28
[ RAJYA SABHA ]
28
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

lynching should be at the very core of this Report. How long should we be witnessing 
people getting into the manholes and cleaning the gutters in inhuman conditions and 
remain spectators of some of them dying in the process? We need to address this as 
a national priority. Two, financial accountability - analyzing budgetary allocations and 
the ground level impact of affirmative action and schemes for empowerment of 
disadvantaged sections. Three, socio-economic gaps - examine the disparities in 
education, employment, median income and poverty levels across different social 
segments.  Why is it that we still have huge backlog in the employment of reserved 
category persons and what needs to be done? Why is it that that there still exists a 
huge gap in educational levels of children coming from disadvantaged categories? 
Number four, providing essential services – assess the reach and quality of health 
care and housing for vulnerable communities. It needs to be examined why the 
deprived sections of our society are still getting deprived. Then, political and social 
inclusion - measuring the participation of SCs, STs, OBCs, minorities, women and 
economically weaker sections in political processes from voting to holding offices 
while identifying systemic barriers to social mobility. Accessibility of persons with 
disability should also form part of this Report. I would like to conclude by recalling the 
words of Dr. Ambedkar, “How long can we continue to deny equality in our social and 
economic life? If we continue to deny it for long, we will do so only by putting...”

Need for constitutional clarity on the definition and identification of minority 
communities in the country 
SHRI PAKA VENKATA SATYANARAYANA (Andhra Pradesh) : Thank you,               
Mr. Chairman, Sir, for giving me this opportunity. I wish to draw the attention of this 
august House to an important constitutional policy issue - identification of minorities. 
As and when India attained independence, certain communities had been said to be 
categorized as minorities. Today, after more than seven decades, their number has 
doubled, which can influence so many constitutional bodies in this system of electoral 
reforms.  Originally, the backward classes which were said to be under the category 
of nomadic, semi-nomadic and denotified tribes, which had been very weaker 
sections, which were numerically very less in number and also very less in income, 
which had minimum needs for their daily livelihoods, they were kept under the 
categories of backward classes and other weaker sections. They are the original 
minorities as per the recent statistics and as has been prescribed in the Constitution 
either through their numbers or through their language or through their habitations or 
whatsoever it may be. I would urge the Government, through you, Sir, to once again 
recall the original concept of minority, under what category it comes, what the 
importance of minority is, how it can be defined and what the sections are that can be 
conferred under the minority groups. Once again it has to be examined by the 
concerned Ministry, so that the original backward classes people, who were originally 
weakened by the system, can become strong; policy of social justice can be 
implemented as per the intention of Dr. Babasaheb Ambedkar; and as per the 
intention of Antyodaya, the members who are originally marginalized can have an 
equal status through their upliftment and we can have Viksit Bharat. I urge the 
Government, through you, Sir, to look into it.  Thank you, Sir.

Demand to increase pension under National Social Assistance Programme to elderly 
people Below Poverty Line (BPL) 
SHRI GOLLA BABURAO (Andhra Pradesh) : Mr. Chairman, Sir, through you, I want 
to bring to the notice of the august House a very sorrowful issue that is going on in our 
country. It is an appeal to increase the pension under National Social Assistance 
Programme to elder BPL people. Social care is a crucial part of any Government to 
provide much-needed support to old people who need assistance for their survival 
and daily needs. The old age pension scheme is a significant step toward fulfilment of 
the Directive Principles of State Policy which mandates the Government to undertake 
welfare measures. No doubt, the Government of India is implementing its schemes. It 
is one of the five sub-schemes of National Social Assistance Programme that 
provides monthly pension to BPL senior citizens. But the most unfortunate part is that 
the Government of India is providing just Rs. 200 per month as pension to BPL senior 
citizens of the country. The second painful fact is that the current system of 
determining beneficiaries relies on antiquated methodology that fails to reflect 
present-day socio-economic realities; thereby, excluding a substantial number of 
genuinely eligible individuals. The third painful fact is that the Union Government has 
capped the number of beneficiaries from all States at 3 crore when the country has 
nearly 15 crore senior citizens.  The fourth painful fact is that the Budget for this has 
been stagnant since 2015-16. I am not saying that all 15 crore are eligible. But, as per 
estimates, around 5.1 crore elderly people are under BPL category. So, all those who 
are eligible should be provided financial assistance under the Government of India 
schemes. The Government of India is giving it to only 3 crore people, thereby 
depriving financial assistance to more than 2 crore people. The point is: Rs. 200 given 
by the Government of India is too low; it was last enhanced, more than one decade 
ago. So, there is an urgent need to increase the amount from Rs. 200 to, at least, Rs. 
1,000 per month. Thank you.

[ RAJYA SABHA ]

[ RAJYA SABHA ]

Need for bringing all hospitals in Puducherry under the ambit of Central Government 
Health Scheme (CGHS) 
SHRI S. SELVAGANABATHY (Puducherry) : Sir, today, I rise to draw the urgent 
attention of this august House to a matter of deep concern affecting our senior 
citizens – the Central Government Health Scheme (CGHS) for pensioners as 
implemented in the Union Territory of Puducherry. The issue is that the CGHS was 
envisioned as a lifeline for retired Government servants, ensuring that their medical 
needs are met with dignity and efficiency. However, in Puducherry, the scheme 
currently covers only two empanelled hospitals though there are six medical colleges 
with hospitals. Even JIPMER, which is a Central Government institution, is not in the 
list! This severely restricts access for pensioners, many of whom are aged and frail. 
The present system requires pensioners to first obtain approval from a CGHS Centre 
and then seek treatment only in the approved list of hospitals. This bureaucratic 
process causes delay in medical care which is, particularly, burdensome for elderly 
patients. The serious human impact is this. Just imagine an aged pensioner in large 
cities like Chennai suffering from a sudden cardiac problem or requiring dialysis! 
Instead of going to the nearest hospital, they are forced to search for a CGHS Centre 
and navigate administrative hurdles. It is not just inconvenient; it is a matter of life and 
death. The very purpose of CGHS is defeated if access to timely treatment is 
compromised. Pensioners, who have dedicated their lives in service to the nation, 
should deserve seamless healthcare without procedural delay. The hospitals are not 
interested since the rates, in their opinion, are not remunerative. Further, the rates are 
not revised annually commensurate with the rise in the cost of maintenance. Another 
important reason is that the payments from CGHS are delayed due to procedural and 
administrative problems. The broader vision is that the healthcare is not a privilege; 
but it is a fundamental right. By strengthening CGHS in Puducherry, we would set a 
precedent for other regions where similar gaps exist.  
[ 10 February,2026, ]
33

I strongly urge the Central Government to expand the ambit of CGHS in 
Puducherry by bringing all hospitals in the Union Territory under the empanelled list. 
This would ensure that pensioners can avail treatment at the nearest hospital without 
delay. Thank you.

raised by the hon. Member, Shri S. Selvaganabathy:  Shri A.A. Rahim (Kerala),      
Dr. John Brittas (Kerala), Shrimati Rajathi (Tamil Nadu), Dr. Anil Sukhdeorao Bonde 
(Maharashtra), Dr. Sasmit Patra (Odisha), Shri R. Girirajan (Tamil Nadu),            
Shri Devendra Pratap Singh (Chhattisgarh) and Shri Banshilal Gurjar (Madhya

Need to regulate electronic games and grant official Sports Status to E-sports 
under the PROG Act, 2025 
SHRI KARTIKEYA SHARMA (Haryana):  Sir, I want to address a digital frontier that is 
full of promise as it is of peril. I am talking about the world of electronic gaming. As we 
march towards the vision of a Viksit Bharat in 2047, we must recognize the orange 
economy. Our creative and digital sector is no longer a peripheral industry. It is a vital 
pillar of our one trillion digital economy goal. Currently, India’s creative economy is 
valued at approximately 30 billion dollars, contributing to nearly 8 per cent of our 
working population. However, we must distinguish between two very different realities 
within the sector. On the one hand, we are witnessing the crisis of unregulated 
amateur gaming and its impact. The sprawling nature of unregulated amateur online 
gaming has led to grave repercussions. We all have heard the heart wrenching stories 
of young children driven by gaming addiction descending into spirals of self harm, as 
we saw in incidents that happened recently, the unfortunate incident where three 
young kids lost their lives. Clinical studies now show a direct link between excessive, 
unregulated gaming and the rise of ADHD, acute anxiety and depression amongst our 
children and youth. As a parent, not merely as a Member of Parliament, I request the 
Government to regulate the entire ecosystem from game development to streaming 
and mandate game audits to ensure these digital spaces are safe for our children.  
While we create a safe and a conducive ecosystem, on the other hand, there is a 
potential of professional Esports largely which remains untapped. We have millions of 
serious highly skilled electronic gamers who are passionate about this craft. India now 
boasts of 500 million amateur gamers, the largest gaming audience in the world, and 
34
[ RAJYA SABHA ]
34
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

lakhs of professional gamers. Our gaming market is valued at about 3.7 billion dollars 
right now. It is projected to soar to 10 billion dollars by 2030. Recently, the AVGC 
sector alone, which will require roughly about 2 million skilled professionals by 2030, is 
a great opportunity for our youth, for employment particularly. To unlock the full value 
of gaming design development professional leagues, I urge the Government to grant 
official sports status to Esports as they are also being accepted by international 
competitions, Olympics and other bodies. This will provide our youth with a 
structured, legitimate career path while positioning Bharat as a global leader in the 
digital economy. The Budget’s proposal to set up AVGC content creator labs in 
15,000 secondary schools is the right start for our youth, Yuva Shakti,  in the right 
direction. It is a very important area. Thank you very much for the opportunity.

Demand for restoration of the original 120 km jurisdiction of the Farakka Barrage 
Project Authority 
SHRI MOHAMMED NADIMUL HAQUE (West Bengal) : Sir, the Farakka Barrage is a 
Union project. It is controlled and operated by the Union Government. And yet, when 
West Bengal bears the devastating consequences of land erosion and siltation, the 
Union suddenly claims it has no responsibility. That is why the Farakka Barrage 
Project Authority’s full 120-Kilometre jurisdiction must be restored immediately.   
Sir, in 2005, the Union Government accepted a basic scientific reality that a 
river functions as a single system. Acting on this, it extended the Farakka’s 
jurisdiction to 40 Kilometres upstream and 80 Kilometres downstream. This decision 
was not political but hydrological. Sir, what changed in 2017 was the Union 
Government's willingness to bear responsibility, when Farakka's jurisdiction was cut 
down to 19.4 Kilometres. Erosion and siltation does not stop at notification 
boundaries. The Union made this position explicit in its letter dated 28 th July, 2022 
t
hat the barrage project authority is responsible only for the safety of the barrage. 
Erosion beyond 19.4 kilometers is the State's responsibility. At the same time, the 
Union retains complete control over barrage gates, and the silt regime. This is not 
federalism. This is the Union Government keeping power while exporting damage.

[ RAJYA SABHA ]

coordination; it was withdrawal. No alternative authority was created. No 
compensation was offered.  
Sir, the costs are staggering.  Since 2017, West Bengal has spent Rs. 168.47 
crores on 31 vulnerable stretches and another Rs. 80.14 crores in a single year on just 
14 Kilometres. Nearly 2,800 hectares of fertile land have been lost and properties 
worth Rs. 1,000 crores have been destroyed by the effects of a Centrally-controlled

of the Farakka Barrage Project Authority. Responsibility cannot be sliced away while 
control remains intact. The people of Bengal will not bear this cost.

Need for examination reforms in view of rampant irregularities and paper leak 
SHRI A.D. SINGH (Bihar) : Mr. Chairman, Sir, I wish to raise the following matter of 
urgent public importance, ‘Need for examination reforms in view of rampant 
irregularities and paper leak.’ Sir, in recent years, the credibility of India's examination 
system has been severely undermined by repeated instances of irregularities and 
paper leaks in both Central and State-conducted recruitment and entrance 
examinations. These instances not only disrupt the lives of lakhs of aspirants, but also 
erode public trust in institutions meant to ensure fair and transparent selection 
processes. Several high-profile cases have come to light. The NEET-UG 2024 paper 
leak caused widespread protests across the country, raising serious questions about 
the security of question papers and the role of examination authorities. Similarly, in 
Bihar, the Bihar Public Service Commission (BPSC) and the Teacher Recruitment 
Exam have witnessed repeated paper leaks, leading to cancellations and arrests. In 
Rajasthan, the Rajasthan Eligibility Examination for Teachers (REET) has faced major 
controversies in multiple editions, with evidence of organized cheating racket involving 
officials and coaching centers. States like Uttar Pradesh, Madhya Pradesh and 
Haryana have also faced similar problems in police, teachers and clerk-level 
recruitment exams. The core reasons behind such failure are lack of strong cyber 
security protocols, corruption within examination and printing agencies, outsourcing 
without proper checks and political interference. The absence of a standard for 
examination governance and inadequate use of technology further weakens the 
process. The Government has initiated some steps - the Public Examination 
(Prevention of Unfair Means) Act, 2024 which criminalizes organized cheating and 
recommends penalties, but only in Central sphere. However, more robust measures 
are needed which should also serve as model to States. This includes: Use of end-
to-end encrypted digital question paper delivery, deployment of AI-based surveillance 
in exam centers, Centralized regulations of private printing and logistic agencies, 
establishment of a National Examination Security Authority and holding top officials 
accountable for administrative lapses. Additionally, there is an urgent need for speedy 
trial of offenders, blacklisting of corrupt service providers and transparent grievance 
redressal system for affected candidates. In conclusion, examination reforms are 
critical not only for restoring student’s confidence, but also for safeguarding the 
merit-based recruitment foundation of India's governance and education system.  
Thank you, Sir.

raised by the hon. Member, Shri A.D. Singh:  Shri Gurwinder Singh Oberoi (Jammu

ORAL ANSWERS TO QUESTIONS 
Balagarh Project of Kolkata Port 
*106. SHRI SAMIK BHATTACHARYA: Will the Minister of PORTS, SHIPPING AND 
WATERWAYS be pleased to state: 
(a)  whether Government has approved and initiated implementation of the 
Balagarh Project of Syama Prasad Mookerjee Port, Kolkata for setting up a 
new cargo handling facility on Balagarh Island, about 85 km upstream from 
Kolkata; 
(b)   the objectives of the project, including decongestion of the Kolkata Dock 
System and improvement of inland waterway connectivity; 
42
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

(c)   the present status of land acquisition, environmental and other statutory 
clearances and tendering; 
(d)     the estimated project cost, funding pattern and timelines; and 
(e)  the proposed cargo handling capacity, trade benefits and employment 
potential envisaged upon commissioning? 
THE MINISTER OF STATE IN THE MINISTRY OF PORTS, SHIPPING AND 
WATERWAYS (SHRI SHANTANU THAKUR): (a) to (e) A statement is laid on the 
Table of the House. 
 
Statement 
(a) Yes. 
(b) To accommodate anticipated traffic growth and reduce intra-city cargo 
movement, Syama Prasad Mookerjee Port Authority (SMPA) is developing dedicated 
container and coal berths at Balagarh.The project also seeks to promote cargo 
transportation through National Waterway-1, thereby, strengthening inland waterway 
connectivity and enhancing overall logistics efficiency. 
(c) Land is already available with the SMPA and environmental clearance 
was received on 24.01.2025. Under the Engineering Procurement and Construction 
(EPC) component of the project, the tender for road construction was opened on 
02.02.2026 and the work order for construction of the Road Over Bridge (ROB) has 
been issued on 09.01.2026. 
(d) The estimated cost of the project is ₹539.17 crore including the 
Engineering Procurement and Construction (EPC) component of Rs.131.97 crore and 
Public Private Partnership (PPP) component of ₹407.20 crore. The EPC component 
is to be funded by the Inland Waterways Authority of India, while the PPP component 
is to be financed and implemented by the selected concessionaire. The EPC part of 
project, except dredging part, is scheduled for completion by 31.03.2028. The 
dredging activities will be taken up by IWAI immediately before commencement of 
construction of Jetties by PPP operators. Construction of facilities under the PPP 
component is to be completed within three years from the award of concession. 
(e) The proposed cargo handling capacity under the project is 2.7 million 
metric tonnes per annum (MMTPA). The project is expected to provide trade benefits 
by reducing congestion arising from cargo movement within city areas, lowering 
[ 10 February,2026, ]
43

logistics costs, and facilitating development of an alternate trade hub. The project is 
expected to generate around 2,600 employment opportunities through both direct 
and indirect avenues which will also create significant employment opportunities for 
the local population, thereby contributing to regional socio-economic development.

Regarding the Balagarh Cargo Handling Project, the main objective of this project is 
to de-congest the terminal section of Kolkata by shifting operations to Balagarh. The 
total land area is 900 acres. Out of this, 300 acres are under the Syama Prasad 
Mookerjee Port. Within that, 100 acres will be utilized for terminal operations, and the 
remaining 200 acres will be used for jetty handling and related activities. Regarding 
the question asked   by the hon. Member about the communication issues; the road 
connectivity has already been sanctioned. The Railway connectivity will be addressed 
in the second phase. Additionally, all environmental clearances have been obtained.

THE MINISTER OF PORTS, SHIPPING AND WATERWAYS (SHRI SARBANANDA 
SONOWAL): Respected Chairman, Sir, hon. Member, Shri Purkayastha, has raised 
a very important question. This is regarding the initiatives undertaken by the Ministry 
of Ports, Shipping and Waterways for the seamless movement of various kinds of 
vessels, cargo vessels, passenger vessels and cruise vessels, in National 
Waterways-16 on River Barak. The stretch on River Barak from Lakhipur to Bhanga is 
121 kilometres long. For the development of this particular length of the river, we have 
started dredging. We have renovated the terminal at Badarpur and Karimgunj. Almost 
50 lakh people reside in the Barak Valley of Assam. This terminal is not just for Assam 
but for nearby States like Mizoram, Manipur and Tripura as well. If the Barak River is 
developed, then, all the States would be benefited. In particular, our farmers, 
entrepreneurs and people in general would be benefited by way of transportation of 
their finished products from one part to the other part with very less expenditure. 
Logistics costs, in particular, would be reduced through movement over the Inland 
Waterways. I believe, that is the benefit it would provide. All this is happening only 
because of the visionary Prime Minister, Shri Narendra Modi, as he loves the country, 
he loves the North Eastern part of India and because of his special initiatives, untiring 
efforts and special support, the entire North East, in the last eleven years, has got a 
special status. Now, almost five crore people in this part of the country have come up 
with self-confidence because of the hon. Prime Minister’s leadership. The entire

[ RAJYA SABHA ]

MR. CHAIRMAN: Question No.107. 
Replacement of ammonia with green hydrogen 
*107. SHRI DIGVIJAYA SINGH: Will the Minister of NEW AND RENEWABLE ENERGY 
be pleased to state: 
(a)   the quantum of Green Hydrogen specifically earmarked for fertilizer use given 
that National Green Hydrogen Mission targets 5 million tonnes of Green 
Hydrogen by 2030, while only 0.5 million tonnes would replace all imported 
Ammonia for Phosphatic fertilizers; 
(b)   the auction-wise details of winning bidders, discovered prices and committed 
offtakersin the 13 planned Green Ammonia auctions aggregating 7.24 lakh 
MT/year; and 
(c)   the reduction in Green House Gas emission likely to be achieved in the 
fertilizerproduction sector by replacing 2.5MMT of imported Grey Ammonia 
with Green Hydrogen? 
THE MINISTER OF NEW AND RENEWABLE ENERGY (SHRI PRALHAD  
JOSHI): (a) to (c) A statement is laid on the Table of the House. 
 
Statement 
 (a) and (b) The Government of India is implementing the National Green 
Hydrogen Mission (NGHM), with an objective to make India a global hub of 
production, usage and export of Green Hydrogen and its derivatives. India’s Green 
Hydrogen production capacity is likely to reach 5 Million Metric Tonnes per annum by 
2030. 
 Strategic Interventions for Green Hydrogen Transition (SIGHT) Programme, is 
a key component of the Mission, which provides incentives for production of green 
hydrogen and electrolyser manufacturing. Scheme guidelines for implementation of 
the incentive scheme for green ammonia production and supply (under mode 2A) 
have been issued under SIGHT Programme. 
 Solar Energy Corporation of India (SECI), has discovered prices through 
competitive bids for the production and supply of 7,24,000 tonnes per annum of 
52
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

Green Ammonia to 13 fertilizer units across India. This green ammonia capacity 
corresponds to 1,27,786 tonnes per annum of green hydrogen production, in 
accordance with the equivalence factor of 0.1765 kg green hydrogen per kg of green 
ammonia. 
 The auction-wise details of the 13 green ammonia winning bidders, discovered 
prices and committed offtakers, aggregating to 7,24,000 metric tonnes per annum 
(MTPA), are given below:

 (c) The estimated reduction in green house gas emissions due to replacement 
of 2.5 million tonnes of grey ammonia by green ammonia is approximately 5 million 
tonnes of equivalent CO₂ emissions annually on a life - cycle basis. 
 While the emissions related to productionof imported grey ammonia do not 
occur within India’s territorial boundaries, its substitution with green ammonia 
contributes to global emission reduction, and facilitates the transition to renewable 
energy based fertiliser production.

DR. SASMIT PATRA : Hon. Chairman, Sir, I have a specific question to the hon.

SHRI PRALHAD JOSHI: Sir, as far as green ammonia is concerned, 7.24 lakh MTPA 
is the figure which I mentioned. It is purely for the fertilizers. There are fertilizer 
companies and there are players who are already participating in this entire process of 
producing green ammonia and green hydrogen. The interim procurement will be 
through SECI -- it is floating the bids -- either from fertilizer units or from any private 
people who want to invest as far as production of green ammonia or even green 
hydrogen is concerned. They will produce it. Green hydrogen cannot be transported 
in its direct form. It will be transported in the form of methane or ammonia to those 
who place the order with them. It will be supplied to them. This is a simple system 
and it is quite appreciated by the market also.

Some studies by the CPCB and international research bodies have highlighted that 
excessive use of chemical fertilizers containing nitrogen and phosphorus leads to 
leaching into rivers and is causing eutrophication, oxygen depletion and large scale 
damage to aquatic life, while over application has also resulted in nitrate 
contamination of groundwater which is affecting the drinking water in several regions.  
Further, reports indicate that effluent from fertilizer manufacturing units add to water

Suposhit Bharat Initiative 
*108. SHRIMATI SUMITRA BALMIK: Will the Minister of AYUSH be pleased to state: 
58
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

(a) the details of the Suposhit Bharat initiative and the specific Ayush-based 
dietary interventions (Ayush Aahar) introduced in Anganwadis of Madhya 
Pradesh to combat malnutrition among women and children; 
(b) whether the Ministry is promoting Garbh Sanskar modules in Ayush Health 
and Wellness Centers to ensure the mental and physical well-being of 
pregnant women in rural areas; and 
(c) the data regarding the distribution of AYUSH-64 and other immunity-boosting 
kits to vulnerable populations in the tribal districts of Madhya Pradesh? 
THE MINISTER OF STATE OF THE MINISTRY OF AYUSH (SHRI PRATAPRAO 
JADHAV): (a) to (c) A statement is laid on the Table of the House. 
Statement 
 (a) The Government is addressing malnutrition among women and children 
through Mission Saksham Anganwadi and Poshan 2.0, which includes Poshan 
Abhiyaan, Anganwadi Services and the Scheme for Adolescent Girls, and is 
implemented through Anganwadi Centres across the country, including Madhya 
Pradesh.Under Mission Poshan 2.0, a new strategy has been made for reduction in 
malnutrition and for improved health, wellness and immunity through activities like 
community engagement, outreach, behavioural change and advocacy.Ayush 
healthcare has been given due emphasize in Poshan 2.0 through anganwadi centers 
with a  focus on prevention of diseases and promotion of wellness through Yoga, 
cultivation of medicinal herbs in Poshan Vatikas, and preparation of Ayush 
formulations to address conditions like anaemia. 
 As part of these efforts, 31,709 Poshan Vatikas have been developed in 
Anganwadi Centres in Madhya Pradesh, while more than 3.13 lakh Poshan Vatikas 
have been developed across the country. The Ministry of Ayush participated in 
Poshan Maah and Poshan Pakhwada organized by the Ministry of Women and Child 
Development (MWCD) through its National Ayush Mission (NAM), National 
Institutes, Research Councils and Ayush Colleges across the country, during which 
more than four crore Ayush-based activities were conducted nationwide from 2021-
2025. 
 The Suposhit Gram Panchayat Abhiyaan under Mission Saksham Anganwadi 
and Poshan 2.0 of MWCDlaunched on 26 December 2024, incentivises top 
performing Gram Panchayats to strengthen Anganwadi services and nutrition related 
[ 10 February,2026, ]
59

activities with a focus on measurable improvements in nutritional outcomes of 
children, pregnant women and lactating mothers. However there is no specific 
initiative titled Suposhit Bharat. 
 (b) Yes, under NAM, the service delivery framework of Ayush Health and 
Wellness Centres, now known as Ayushman Arogya Mandirs (Ayush), envisages 
care during pregnancy and childbirth as one of its activities through preventive 
pregnancy care (Garbhini Charya), including appropriate food and lifestyle practices 
for the woman and family, specific dietary preparations, use of home remedies, and 
Yoga. 
 Further Supraja program (Ayush Maternal and Neonatal Intervention) under 
NAM is a specialized initiative aimed at promoting maternal and neonatal health 
outcomes by integrating Ayush-based preventive, promotive, and therapeutic care 
into existing public health systems. 
 (c) Yes, as per the data received from Directorate of Ayush, Government of 
Madhya Pradesh immunity-boosting kits were distributed to vulnerable populations in 
all 51 districts of Madhya Pradesh, including 20 tribal districts. The kits contained 
AYUSH-64 and other immunity-boosting Ayush medicines, such as Ayush Kadha, 
TrikatuChurna, Sanshamani Vati, Anu Taila, Arsenicum Album and 
KhameeraMarwareed. The details are enclosed as Annexure. 
 Also the Central Council for Research in Ayurvedic Sciences (CCRAS), an 
autonomous organization under Ministry of Ayush is providing Ayurveda Health care 
services in tribal dominant areas across the country at the door steps, through the 
Tribal Health Care Research Programme (THCRP) under the Development Action 
Plan for Scheduled Tribes (DAPST) through 14 CCRAS institutes in 14 states, 
including Madhya Pradesh state. Under this programme, Ayuraksha Kits containing 
Chyawanprash, Ayush Kwatha, Samshamani Vati, and Anu Taila were distributed by 
the CCRAS-Regional Ayurveda Research Institute (RARI), Gwalior, in Scheduled 
Tribe-dominated areas of Chhindwara district, specifically Block Tamia, Madhya 
Pradesh, during the financial year 2021-22.

MR. CHAIRMAN: Now, Q. No. 109. 
Benefits of U-Win Portal 
#*109. SHRI MITHLESH KUMAR : Will the MINISTER OF HEALTH AND FAMILY 
WELFARE be pleased to state: 
(a)  the total number of children and pregnant women registered on the U-WIN 
portal; 
(b) whether QR code vaccination certificates were issued for every vaccine 
dose in 2025; 
(c)   if so, the total number of such certificates issued; 
(d)  the number of SMS reminder messages sent to people in rural areas about 
upcoming vaccine doses; 
(e) whether U-WIN helped identify children who had never received any 
vaccine; and 
(f)  if so, the number of such “zero-dose” children who were brought under 
vaccination? 
THE MINISTER OF STATE IN THE MINISTRY OF HEALTH AND FAMILY  
WELFARE (SHRIMATI ANUPRIYA PATEL): (a) to (f) A Statement is laid on the Table 
of the House.  
Statement  
  (a) As on 3 rd February 2026, 11.05 crore children and 3.75 crore pregnant 
women are registered on U-WIN portal. 
 (b) and (c) Every beneficiary can download the QR code based vaccination 
certificate from U-WIN portal. In the year 2025, 8.01 crore beneficiaries were 
registered in U-WIN and the digital QR code based certificates were made available to 
them. 
 (d) A total of 29.42 crore SMSs including reminder SMSs were sent to 
beneficiaries in 2025 from U-WIN portal. 
 
# Original notice of the question was received in Hindi. 
64
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

 (e) and (f) U-win has helped in line-listing of children due for vaccination. 
These children are digitally mapped on the portal for regular follow-ups with SMS 
alerts for ensuring their due doses. These children as identified also by the front line 
workers, are provided their due vaccination which is duly recorded on U-WIN platform 
for ensuring completion of their routine immunization.

[ RAJYA SABHA ]

DR. KANIMOZHI NVN SOMU (Tamil Nadu) : Thank you, hon. Deputy Chairman, Sir, 
for this opportunity to speak here. At the outset, I would say that this Budget is yet 
another failed Budget by the hon. Finance Minister. We have to appreciate her for her 
consistency. Her consistency to fail in nine consecutive times is a record. Her 
consistency is so good for herself, for her party *but not for the country. Sir, in the 
last few years, when the Budget speeches echo Thirukkural, the people of my State 
console themselves, at least our language has found a voice, even if our State has not 
found any space in the Budget allocation. But this year, even that symbolic 
acknowledgement is absent, leaving the people of my State feeling both physically 
neglected and culturally unheard. So, Sir, I start my Budget speech by quoting 
Thirukkural.  &“It is not the javelin that gives victory to a king, but his sceptre when he 
rules with justice.” This means, Sir, power may win battles, but justice wins hearts 
and stability. This Union Budget 2026-27 is anti-poor and detached from the 
country's economic realities. This Budget is faceless, baseless and visionless. This 
Budget offers no relief to workers, farmers, salaried middle-class people or the 
unemployed, while continuing to prioritize the big corporate interests. Sir, this Budget 
exposes the blind commitment of the BJP-NDA Government to a handful of corporate 
houses. The Government cutting subsidies in critical sectors is very bad. The cuts in 
the food, fertiliser and petroleum subsidies amount to a direct assault on the workers 
and peasants. Transfer to States has fallen by lakhs of crores, undermining fiscal 
federalism.  
Sir, what Perarignar Anna said in 1966 is very much relevant about the colossal 
taxation and the meager returns. “What right has this Government to demand more 
and more taxes when their performance is of such a low order? ” I think that this 
Government, after having taxed the people so much, has not given proper returns or 
proper accounts to the nation. Therefore, though I realise that I do not have the 
power to stop it, I can abet a crime of allocating colossal sums to this insufficient, 
inefficient, unrealistic, unresponsive and undemocratic Government that is being 
carried on. But, whatever may be the criticism that is offered on this side, they have 
the numbers and the logic and they play the game.” Therefore, Mr. Deputy Chairman, 
Sir, offering this criticism, we have to go to the other forum and receive justice from 
the only source, the first source, the primary source, the public. So, we are confident 
of getting a proper verdict. This Budget would deepen inequality and worsen the 
ongoing economic crisis. The Budget is detached from the concerns of the masses 
 
* Not recorded. 
& English translation of the original speech delivered in Tamil. 
[ 10 February,2026, ]
71

and marked by deliberate vagueness. The Finance Minister has avoided giving 
scheme-wise allocations, calling it an attempt to obscure funding cuts.  
Sir, this Government has failed to stimulate demand despite the Economic 
Survey's emphasis on domestic consumption. On agriculture and employment, the 
Government has ignored demands for a legal MSP and continued to push anti-labour 
policies. It is a betrayal of the people, workers and the federal spirit. Sir, this Budget 
failed to address the basic issues such as inflation and unemployment. The slogan, 
Viksit Bharat , rings hollow as subsidies for food and fertilisers have been cut. It also 
underlined the push for private-led healthcare and education and the focus is only on 
corporate-friendly projects. In 2014, the price of one savaran of gold was Rs. 28,000, 
but in 2026, it is Rs. 1,20,000. That means the price of gold has quadrupled in the last 
12 years. In 2014, the value of the Indian rupee, we all know, against the US dollar 
was 58.25.  On 28 th January, 2026, it was Rs.92.03. And, the price of one litre petrol 
was Rs.71.41/- and that of diesel was Rs.55.49/-. In 2026, the petrol price is 
Rs.102.14/- and the diesel price is Rs.92.51/-. Even the domestic LPG cylinder was 
sold at Rs.410/- in 2014, but now in 2026, it is Rs.853/-, breached Rs.1,000/- two 
years back. Sir, the price of the essential commodities skyrocketed in the last 12 
years and several products have gone beyond the reach of the common people. The 
hon. Ministers of this Government, including the Prime Minister, believe that 
announcing about the Government decisions on the X platform is more important than 
announcing it on the floor of this august House. Such a disrespect to the Parliament, 
Sir! 
Sir, tariff reduction is not reciprocal. India has been tied into a monitoring 
system on its energy choices by the US, as per an order by President Trump. India-

room for growth, welfare and support to States. Overall, this Budget shows clear

SHRI SUKHENDU SEKHAR RAY (West Bengal) : Sir, I thank you for giving me this 
opportunity. While we are discussing the Union Budget 2026, my mind goes back to 
ancient Indian philosopher who lived in 600 BC. His name was Charvaka; known as a 
non-believer as he rejected the tenets of Vedas and advocated for materialism as the 
means of which one lives in the world. One great shloka propagated by Charvaka 
was, "+g˛ ][gb˛ )%/ ][gb˛ ,×o/ \ ﬂ g+ घﬂ&/ !’b˛" Meaning that so long you live, live 
in happiness; take a loan to consume ghee. To me, the functionaries of the 
Government look like Charvaka as it appears that the total Government loan including 
external debt is estimated at Rs. 200.53 lakh crore which signifies the Amrit Kaal.   
Sir, let me quickly look at a few points from the Budget. There is no change in 
Income Tax slabs from the previous year though it is claimed that very soon, India will 
be the third economically developed country in the comity of nations. 100 per cent FDI 
has been allowed in the insurance sector of India, endangering the business being 
done by nationalised insurance companies. The Government has finalised IDBI for 
strategic sale which has 30.40 per cent share of the Government and 30.24 per cent 
share of LIC. What will be the fate of other nationalised banks? I want to know from 
the Government.  
84
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

Sir, tax holiday has been announced until 2047 to foreign companies in 
selected sectors. Further, the tax holiday for IFC units and offshore banking units, 
infamous as custodians of black money, has been increased from 10 to 20 years. In 
the name of ease of doing business, between 2024 and 2025, the Government has 
repealed 1,562 laws to eliminate 40,000 compliance requirements. Why? We need 
proper explanation. In the same period, over 3,700 legal provisions in different Acts 
have been decriminalized, replacing imprisonment with monetary penalties. My 
question is: What about ease of living?  How many enactments have been made by 
the Government to combat the precarious uncertain future being confronted by the 
vast army of unemployed youth in particular, and other jobless citizens in general, 
keeping in view that Article 21 of the Constitution guarantees all citizens to live with 
human dignity? It is laughable that Government has only assured to set up a high-
powered committee to look into prospects for education to employment and 
enterprise. Our experience is that committees and commissions mean mortuaries.

dustbin. …(Interruptions)…  But it has been done away with now. For the past few

3.00 P.M. 
 It is a matter of great concern that the climatic condition of entire country is 
going to be unbearable due to rise of unprecedented floods, frequent cyclones, and

received patronage for construction of port city.

super rich controls approximately 40 per cent to 51.5 per cent of the total national 
wealth; top 10 per cent rich owns about 65 per cent to 66 per cent of the total national 
wealth; 40 per cent middle-class holds roughly 23 per cent of the national wealth;

11 per cent of national wealth. The top 10 per cent holding 65 per cent of wealth

Sir, with the introduction of Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code, the bank 
fraudsters and willful defaulters have been favoured with an exit route to shirk their 
loan liabilities.  The NCLT is allowing even 40 per cent to 98 per cent haircut of the 
total dues in the resolution proceedings. The Chief Justice of India had commented in 
a case on 4 th February, 2026, and I quote, “Unfortunately, the Insolvency and 
Bankruptcy Code is being misused. You get the company and assets undervalued.  
Then you indulge in a kind of an auction which is also planned.  Friends or family 
come and buy. Valuation of the assets would not be even 1 per cent of the market 
value. Every day I am observing this."  Why the Government does not scrap this code 
brought at the instance of the lobby of crony capitalists?

under control, I am afraid that the days are not too far when country will be gulfed into

MR. DEPUTY CHAIRMAN: Shri Rajinder Gupta, not present. Shri Meda Raghunadha 
Reddy.                                                     
SHRI MEDA RAGHUNADHA REDDY (Andhra Pradesh) : Sir, the Union Budget 2026-
27 has been presented at a time when India’s economic fundamentals remain stable. 
The Government revenues are strong and with a total expenditure of about Rs.53.5 
lakh crore and a fiscal deficit target of 4.3 per cent of GDP, the Budget reflects 
responsible and balanced financial management. India’s growth is largely 
consumption-driven, contributing nearly 60 per cent to GDP. Sustaining this growth 
requires balancing fiscal discipline with taxpayer relief and adequate liquidity for 
businesses. In this context, I wish to raise three key concerns. First, unmet 
expectations of the middle class. Second, continuing ease-of doing business 
 
& English translation of the original speech delivered in Bengali. 
* Not recorded. 
[ 10 February,2026, ]
89

challenges and third, policy measures required to fully harness India’s demographic 
opportunity, which will peak around 2041. I come to individual taxation and missing 
middle-class relief. Despite rising personal income tax collections, the Budget has 
retained the existing slab structure. This has resulted in a fiscal drag, where inflation 
pushes taxpayers into higher tax brackets without any real increase in purchasing 
power. Middle-income taxpayers, particularly, those earning around Rs.15 lakh 
annually, are under pressure from rising housing, healthcare, and education costs. 
However, the expected increase in the basic exemption limit or widening of lower tax 
slabs has not been addressed. The highest tax rate of 30 per cent, which effectively 
rises to nearly 40 per cent after surcharge, applies at relatively modest income levels. 
Many experts have suggested raising this threshold to encourage compliance and 
domestic investment. Similarly, while employment-related expenses have increased, 
the Standard Deduction remains unchanged at Rs.50,000 under the Old Regime and 
Rs.75,000 under the New Regime. A revision to Rs.1 lakh would have provided 
meaningful relief. While the New Tax Regime promotes simplicity, it excludes key 
deductions related to savings, health insurance and housing. As a result, simplicity 
has come at the cost of long-term financial security. A progressive Standard 
Deduction or selective inclusion of core social security deductions would have struck 
a better balance.   
Sir, on ease of doing business, while the Budget focuses on fiscal 
consolidation, it falls short on ease of doing business concerns faced by 
entrepreneurs who are expected to drive India’s growth over the next two decades.  
A major gap is the absence of a one-time mechanism to resolve long-pending tax 
disputes. Over 5.49 lakh tax appeals remain pending, locking up large sums of 
business capital.  Industry had expected a settlement or amnesty-type scheme, but 
the Budget offers only limited procedural relief.   
On GST implementation, while collections have improved significantly, there 
are instances where some field-level officials created unnecessary hurdles by issuing 
repeated summons and harassing dealers. Clear instructions are needed to ensure a 
facilitative and trust-based approach. Further, under GST notifications relating to 
works contracts, Notification No.1/2018, a concessional 5 per cent rate applies to 
Government contracts where the earthwork component exceeds 75 per cent, 
including subcontractors. However, in practice, this benefit is often denied to 
subcontractors engaged by other subcontractors, despite the nature of work being 
identical. This results in an illogical solution where a subcontractor collector collects 5 
per cent GST but is forced to pay 18 per cent to the next level, creating undue 
90
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

financial stress. I urge the Government to issue clear instructions to uniformly extend 
the concessional rate to all eligible subcontractors. Sir, further, non-corporate 
MSMEs such as proprietorships, partnerships and LLPs face effective tax rates of 
over 31 per cent, compared to 25 per cent for corporate manufacturing entities. This 
lack of tax parity discourages reinvestment and pushes firms to restructure for tax 
reasons rather than efficiency. Targeted income tax relief and fairer treatment would 
directly support job creation and grassroots growth.   
On demographic dividend- the 2047 opportunity -- Sir, India is at a critical 
juncture where the next twenty years represent a one-time opportunity to convert our 
demographic advantage into sustained economic growth. Nearly 65 per cent of our 
population is below the age of 35 years and the working-age population is expected 
to peak by 2041. The Government employment alone cannot generate nearly one 
crore jobs required annually. Therefore, policy must focus on empowering young 
entrepreneurs, particularly in high-growth sunrise sectors. India’s ambitions in 
renewable energy and semiconductors are welcome. …(Time-bell rings)…  Sir, I will 
take one minute from the time allocated to my party. 
MR. DEPUTY CHAIRMAN: Please conclude. 
SHRI MEDA RAGHUNADHA REDDY : However, growth must extend beyond 
metropolitan start-ups to mass entrepreneurship across Bharat. This requires 
stronger credit guarantee schemes, simpler regulatory exits and better integration of 
MSMEs from Tier-2 and Tier-3 cities into global value chains. …(Time-bell rings)…  
MR. DEPUTY CHAIRMAN: Please conclude. 
SHRI MEDA RAGHUNADHA REDDY : A long-term entrepreneurship dividend 
roadmap, aligned with the vision of Viksit Bharat 2047, is needed to support regional 
growth clusters and promote innovation, research and skill development.  With these 
observations, on behalf of YSRCP Party, I support the Union Budget, 2026-27.  
Thank you, Sir.                   
MR. DEPUTY CHAIRMAN: Shri Manas Ranjan Mangaraj.  
SHRI MANAS RANJAN MANGARAJ (Odisha) : &“Hon. Chairman, Sir, we have a 
proverb in Odia: "Money is not in hand, yet the mind is set on the son's marriage."  
This saying naturally comes to mind while discussing the Budget presented by the 
 
& English translation of the original speech delivered in Odia. 
[ 10 February,2026, ]
91

Finance Minister for the financial year 2026-27. The Finance Minister’s clever attempt 
to manage the fiscal deficit may have made the Budget appear attractive on the 
surface, but in reality, it has failed to satisfy any section of society. A fiscal deficit of 
over Rs 18 lakh crore within a massive budget of Rs 53.47 lakh crore exposes the true 
financial condition of the country. How the Government portrays a Budget with a 24 
percent deficit as "excellent" is beyond the imagination of the common man. As an 
Odia, I will not hesitate to say that this Budget has brought great disappointment to 
the people of Odisha. Like 4.5 crore Odias, I too hoped that the 'double engine' 
Government would deliver for the state. It was expected that the Government would 
emphasize on infrastructure, employment opportunities, and additional grants to 
accelerate Odisha's progress. However, I must say with regret that the Union Budget 
has been a complete letdown for Odisha. 
The Budget includes Odisha in the National Waterways Project. Ironically, this 
proposal is a decade old. In 2008, six waterways in Odisha were included in National 
Waterways-5, yet not a single one has been implemented to date. Re-introducing it 
now is like a new cover to a old book. While Odisha’s rivers are gasping on 
"deathbeds" of sand due to the negligence of neighboring states, the Finance 
Minister’s dream of sailing ships on dry sand is like rubbing salt into the wounds of the 
Odia people. Odisha has also been included in the Rare Earth Corridor. But how will 
Odisha benefit? This corridor will merely facilitate the extraction and easy 
transportation of mineral resources—coal, iron ore, bauxite, and chromite—out of 
the State. The people of Odisha will be left with nothing but dust, smoke and 
pollution. Given that coal royalties have not been revised for years and the Centre 
maintains an inconsistent policy on green tax, there is no reason to believe this will 
benefit Odisha in any way. 
Sir, the budget announced seven High-Speed Railway Corridors, yet Odisha 
did not receive a single one. Not even one corridor will pass through the State. 
Furthermore, Odisha has been neglected in the Buddhist Circuit Development Plan. 
Ratnagiri, Udayagiri, and Lalitgiri—known as the "Diamond Triangle" of Buddhist 
tourism—have been completely ignored.  While the Government announced support 
for handlooms and Khadi, the GST on handicrafts and handlooms—the lifeline for 
lakhs of weavers in Odisha—has not been reduced. Additionally, Odisha finds no 
place in the development plan for 200 industrial clusters. When the BJP came to 
power in 2014, it promised Special Category Status for Odisha. Yet, even after 12 
years, the Central Government remains silent; the issue is not even mentioned in this 
budget. The repeated demand for a disaster-resilient ecosystem for Odisha, which 
92
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

faces regular natural calamities, has been ignored. The State has been completely 
deprived of proposed chemical parks, hi-tech tool rooms, eco-friendly freight 
corridors, container production units, and programs to enhance coconut and cashew 
production. 
Sir, the entire country was watching this Budget. However, the Finance 
Minister did not utter a single word for the common man during her 85-minute 
speech. The middle class hoping for income tax relief, the youth seeking 
employment, and the common citizens praying for price reductions have all been 
deeply disappointed. While the 2025-26 Budget seemed tailored for the Bihar 
elections, many now feel this Budget is focused on the upcoming elections in West 
Bengal, Kerala, and Tamil Nadu. In a federal system, the development of the state 
should be the priority for the nation's growth; unfortunately, the "Development of the 
East" (Purvodaya) appears to be confined only to speeches. Thank you, Sir.”

MR. DEPUTY CHAIRMAN: Shri Ajit Kumar Bhuyan.  
SHRI AJIT KUMAR BHUYAN (Assam): Thank you, Mr. Deputy Chairman, Sir, for 
giving me the opportunity to speak on the Union Budget 2026-27.  
“PhMF LJUFMF H[ eKVF Mहs, 
hV HVH ?  LJUFMF lhHF H[ @HPJ Mहs^” 
Mr. Deputy Chairman, Sir, the Budget, 2026-27 is anti-youth, anti-farmers, 
anti-women and anti-workers. I can only say that this Budget will bring rural distress 
and unemployment of unimaginable levels. Sir, the border and hill districts across the 
country have been facing high levels of unemployment among the youth, but the 
Budget document has not envisaged any roadmap for addressing the concern of 
these areas. I have my own doubt that the youth of that sensitive area may be lured 
by the hostile neighbouring countries against the interests of the nation. 
Sir, I will not dwell upon the issue of core importance, that is, conflict in the 
naxalism-affected areas and Kashmir, as many learned speakers have spoken about 
poor allocation in the Budget for the same. Being the lone Opposition voice from the 
North-East Region in this August House, I would like to highlight the false promises 
made to the people of the North-East. The promises ring hollow when immediate 
concerns remain unaddressed. Assam is yet to receive Rs. 27,000 crore from the 
Centre for flood and disaster management. In a State where floods are a yearly 
headache and thereby roads deteriorate rapidly, the Budget has just remained silent 
and the people of Assam have been left to their destiny to face river bank erosion, 
which displaces lakhs of vulnerable and poor people.  Sir, with barely two months left 
[ 10 February,2026, ]
97

for the Assembly election in Assam, the Union Budget 2026-27 has triggered 
disappointment across the State. No major State-specific initiative has been taken. 
But, the ruling incumbents at both the Centre and the State are hoping to get massive 
mandate. Are people of Assam so ignorant? The unjust approach of Modi 
Government will certainly oust them from the seat of power in this country. This 
Budget document reveals a sovereign truth. The Ministry of DoNER has received an 
allocation of just about Rs.6,812 crore, which is barely 0.1 per cent of the total Central 
expenditure of Rs.54 lakhs crore. This exposes the hollow claim of a special love for 
this region of the Modi Government. Successive Budgets presented by the Modi 
Government in the last 12 years have brought economic isolation because core 
connectivity projects remain under-funded. The introduction of a Buddhist Circuit for 
the North-Eastern States has been pitched as a major transformative initiative. But, I 
am sorry to say that no specific Budget and timetable have been reflected alongside 
the announcement. I am of the strong view that in the absence of concrete financial 
backing, tourism-centric rhetoric may not help the region's deeper economic issues 
such as unemployment, industrial stagnation or youth migration. Across the North-
East, significant socio-economic problems, including agricultural distress, youth 
unemployment and persistent infrastructure gaps remain unaddressed. I will not take 
the valuable time of the House and conclude by apprising the Government not to test 
the people of the North-East Region in  the laboratory of £and false and redress the 
whole concern in the utmost manner to the expectation of the region.  
MR. DEPUTY CHAIRMAN : Please use parliamentary words. ’ )/)[ „ \+ 
&b+$ \[!]q Do not use unparliamentary words, please. That will not go on 
record.  
SHRI AJIT KUMAR BHUYAN: I am concluding. The political conflict of the region will 
get further deeper (Time-bell rings) if geographical and economic disparities will keep 
haunting the region. Thank you.

SHRIMATI JEBI MATHER HISHAM (Kerala) : Sir, I thank my Party for giving me the 
opportunity to speak on the Budget. On the 1 st of February, 2026, on a freezing 
Sunday morning, we waited with high expectations, the nation waited in hope, the 
stock exchange kept fingers crossed. Three days before, the Economic Survey was 
tabled with key challenges in it. We expected, the nation expected, the stock 
exchange expected that the identified challenges in the Economic Survey would be 
addressed by the Finance Minister in her Budget Speech.Then came the much-
awaited moment. We heard Madam Finance Minister speak for 85 minutes; 15,623 
words were spoken. We heard words like first kartavya, second kartavya, third 
kartavya, sankalp, yuvashakti, viksit Bharat, fiscal prudence. We heard phrases like 
‘action over ambivalence’, ‘reform over rhetoric’, ‘people over populism’, 
atmanirbharta. But, Sir, we did not hear Economic Survey 2025-26. Nation did not 
hear challenges outlined in the Economic Survey. We saw stock market crashing, 
stooping all time low, immediately after the Finance Minister's Budget Speech.  
Nation did not hear about the capital investment, unemployment issue, issue of slow 
growth rate or slow fiscal consolidation. We heard, the nation heard all of these issues 
which were outlined in the Economic Survey, from the hon. former Finance Minister.  
Shri P. Chidambaram spoke at length about these issues. Randeep Surjewala ji 
brought to light in detail false announcements versus media hype, the reality of zero 
allotment and zero spending, the fund cuts in crucial sectors and how the rural poor 
and poor farmers stand ignored in this Budget. Neeraj Dangi ji highlighted the play of 
102
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

numbers in this Budget, how the youth, the SCs/STs, laborers stand ignored in this 
Budget.  
Sir, I want to add a few concerns. The Budget was presented on 1 st of 
February. On 2nd February, in the midnight hours, when India was sleeping, President 
Trump announced to the world the US-India trade deal. President Trump dictated 
India to lower trade barriers, to stop purchase of Russian oil, to buy oil from US and 
Venezuela. President Trump did a shout-out. Prime Minister Modi also committed to 
buying more than 500 billion US dollars worth of US energy, technology, agricultural 
and other products -- all in the midnight hour, Sir. We remember the words of our 
first Prime Minister, Pandit Jawaharlal Nehru, spoken at the midnight in the Tryst with 
Destiny. “At the stroke of the midnight hour, when the world sleeps, India will awake                                                  
to    life    and    freedom.” And now, it is ‘tyrst with destiny’.* 
 And after the whole world came to know, our Prime Minister tweeted. I quote, 
“Wonderful to speak with my dear friend President Trump. Delighted that Made in 
India products will now have a reduced tariff of 18 per cent. Big thanks to President 
Trump on behalf of 1 billion people of India for this wonderful announcement." Sir, 
please note, the word ‘wonderful’ was used twice in one tweet. *I respectfully ask 
the Prime Minister and the Finance Minister: Is it really wonderful? Is it really 
wonderful to lower the trade barriers to the US? Is it really wonderful to surrender our 
freedom to decide from whom we should buy oil? Is it really wonderful to give a 
whopping commitment of 500 billion dollars worth of US energy, technology, 
agriculture and other products? Will it be really wonderful for our farmers? I 
respectfully ask the Prime Minister, ‘Is it really wonderful to have 6 th February Order by 
the US President which says that Washington will establish a US Monitoring 
Committee tasked with overseeing India's implementation of the oil prohibition and 
violation?’ I respectfully ask the Prime Minister, ‘Isn't this US Monitoring Committee 
mechanism kick-starting precedence for foreign oversight of India's policy 
decisions?’ I respectfully ask the Prime Minister, ‘Isn't this marking the beginning of a 
rare instance of American oversight on India's energy sourcing decisions and clear 
attack on India's sovereignty?’ Are we a rogue nation to be monitored? I respectfully 
ask the Prime Minister, ‘Isn’t this Presidential Order by your so-called ‘wonderful 
friend’ effectively surrendering India’s energy security to America’s Presidential 
whims?’ Our Prime Minister said ‘wonderful’ twice for this. The nation would like to 
know this. The nation has the right to know this.  Just as I remember Nehru ji, I also 
 
* Not recorded. 
[ 10 February,2026, ]
103

remember another Prime Minister, who said this many years ago to the US. The 
Prime Minister said, ‘You send your 7 th Fleet across the seven seas. We will see your 
fleet here. Neither your fleet nor your Captain of the fleet will go from here.’ That is 
courage. She is Indira Gandhi who is remembered as the Iron Lady of India.   
Now let me speak about my home State, Kerala. The Union Budget once again 
has shown complete neglect towards Kerala. Kerala was expecting AIIMS. But there 
is no AIIMS. Kerala was expecting high speed rail. But there is no high speed rail.  No 
allocation for the long-cherished Nilambur-Nanjangud Railway Line. Kerala was 
expecting second development phase of Vizhinjam Port.  But there is no approval, no 
allocation. Kerala was expecting special project for rubber. But there is nothing for 
rubber. Kerala was expecting special project to reduce the challenge of man-animal 
conflict and projects to counter agricultural loss. But there is nothing for that. Kerala 
was expecting project for modern paddy storage. But there is nothing for that.   
Wayanad MP Priyanka Gandhi ji has been continuously requesting for 
Wayanad. But request for Wayanad package still remains unfulfilled. Kerala was 
expecting Pravasi Rehabilitation. There is nothing for that. Kerala was expecting a 
Special Package of Rs.21,000 crore considering the loss caused to the GSDP and 
reduced borrowing limit. But there is nothing for that. And what did Kerala receive?  
Kerala received aamma, the sea turtle. Newspapers are a reflection of the mood of 
the State. See this, Sir. It is not atmanirbhar. It is aamma nirbhar. This I want to 
authenticate.   
cK\d>8 (t>. Lu_Ax x;l): ’ )b display & \[!]q 
SHRIMATI JEBI MATHER HISHAM : Sir, Kerala received rare earth corridor in Kollam 
and Alappuzha. But, for that, we have our concerns whether it will pose grave threat 
to the ecology, Kerala's fragile ecosystem and livelihood. Fisherfolk and coastal 
communities have concerns. Also, there is a concern whether this is exploitation to 
protect corporate interest masqueraded as policy. We often hear the Prime Minister, 
the Home Minister and the Finance Minister speaking about cooperative federalism.  
In reality, Kerala experiences not cooperative federalism, but only non-cooperative 
federalism. Kerala has not received its fair share. This is not cooperative federalism, 
but rather denigrative federalism. Kerala is treated not as a partner but as a political 
inconvenience by the Union Government. BJP-led NDA Government does not 
practise constitutional federalism. It practises punitive federalism. This discrimination 
is not accidental. It is structured, deliberate and institutionalized. So, it is not 
104
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

cooperative federalism. It is frustrative federalism. Kerala stands virtually erased from 
the Union Budget, 2026-27. None of the request proposals made by Kerala was 
considered. There is zero consideration for it. So it is not cooperative federalism. It is 
non-operative federalism. I conclude this by saying that development funds are not 
political favours. They are the constitutional rights. And Kerala is not asking for 
charity. Kerala is demanding fair share. We can only speculate if Kerala’s share has 
been allocated to the 500-billion dollar surrender to America. We had local body 
elections a month ago.

Next is Shri Abdul Wahab, four minutes. 
SHRI ABDUL WAHAB (Kerala): Thank you, Sir, for giving me an opportunity to speak 
on the Budget. It is very sad, disappointment for us, for Kerala, for Nilambur and all, 
and especially for minorities. Whatever was promised, we thought that after winning a 
few seats in the Thiruvananthapuram Corporation by the BJP in Kerala, there would 
be some sort of a gift from BJP. Even Rajeev Chandrasekhar ji, our former Minister, 
said in Kerala: “BJP is also disappointed”. Then, what will be our situation? So, they 
did not consider Kerala anywhere. Regarding minorities, whatever was available 
before, is not there. The Maulana Azad scholarship was revoked completely and 
totally. Whatever we were getting in some other areas also, we are not getting or it is 
not passed. Even though there is Budget provision, it is not spent. The State will say 
this about the Centre and the Centre will say about the State. Whatever projects were 
offered are not coming because of Kerala Government's negligence. The Kerala 
Government is not properly giving Utilization Certificate to the Government of India.  
So, in that way, Kerala Government is also doing the same thing as the Government 
of India is doing. We are in a difficult situation. So many projects for minorities, which 
the Centre allowed or was going to give, the Kerala Government is not giving the 
money that they have got. So, we are totally in between the devil and the deep sea, 
the Central Government and State Government.  …(Interruptions)…   
DR. V. SIVADASAN (Kerala): Sir, he is telling wrong things.   
SHRI ABDUL WAHAB: I would only tell wrong things according to my colleagues.  
They would tell everything right. That is what is happening nowadays. They are just 
blaming the Centre, the Centre is blaming Kerala and we are in between. So, I would 
urge the Minister for Finance..…(Interruptions)…   
DR. V. SIVADASAN: Sir, please ask him to authenticate it.   
SHRI ABDUL WAHAB: I would authenticate later.  …(Interruptions)…  I would 
authenticate it. I had my own project which was brought by the Prime Minister as PM 
Jan Vikas Karyakaram (PMJVK). With my application, whatever they got for Vadakara 
Hospital -- Rs.80 crores from Government of India -- not a single rupee is  
spent.  …(Interruptions)…  No, no, it is 100 per cent.  …(Interruptions)… It is not 
without evidence. I can give you and you can go into the details about Vadakara. 
They are forcing me to say all this otherwise, I would keep it  
normal. …(Interruptions)…

Next, Shri Debashish Samantaray – hon. Member not present. Then, Shri Masthan 
Rao Yadav Beedha. You have seven minutes.   
[ 10 February,2026, ]
113

SHRI MASTHAN RAO YADAV BEEDHA (Andhra Pradesh) : Thank you, Vice-
Chairman, Sir, for giving me this opportunity. On behalf of Telugu Desam party and 
our leader and Andhra Pradesh Chief Minister, hon. Nara Chandrababu Naidu, I rise 
to wholeheartedly support the Budget 2026-27. It reflects our commitment to 
achieving a Viksit Atmanirbhar Bharat  by 2047. There has been a clear increase in 
public capital expenditure from Rs. 2 lakh crore in 2014-15 to Rs. 12.2 lakh crore in 
the financial year 2026-27. This strong infrastructure push will have a definite GDP 
multiplier effect. The fiscal deficit is estimated at 4.3 per cent of GDP in 2026-27, 
down from 4.4 per cent in 2025-26. The debt-to-GDP ratio is estimated at 55.6 per 
cent in 2026-27, compared to 56.1 per cent in 2025- 26.                                                             
Tax on income has increased from Rs.14,38,000/- crores to Rs.14,66,000/- crores, 
indicating a sense of confidence people have in the Government. These figures show 
that the NDA Government is balancing national growth with fiscal responsibility. This 
Budget strengthens India's economic foundation towards growth, inviting confidence 
in our future.  
Respected Vice-Chairman, Sir, as regards development and infrastructure, 
the proposed high-speed rail corridors linking Hyderabad-Bengaluru and Hyderabad-
Chennai will significantly improve logistics efficiency. Districts such as Anantapur, 
Kurnool and Nellore in Andhra Pradesh will benefit from faster freight mobility. This will 
support industry, agriculture and regional value chains. The continued emphasis on 
the East Coast Industrial Corridor is timely. I request the Government to consider a 
dedicated East Coast Freight Corridor linking major port cities in my Andhra Pradesh 
State, including Visakhapatnam, Kakinada, and Nellore. This will integrate coastal 
manufacturing clusters with the national and global supply chains. I also thank the 
Government for taking up the Dugarajapatnam Port in Andhra Pradesh under the Ship 
building Development Scheme.   
Now, I come to fisheries and marine economy. Sir, Andhra Pradesh remains 
India's leading fish and shrimp producing and exporting State. Recent trade 
developments, including tariff rationalization with the USA and the EU provide relief to 
aqua farmers and exporters. I thank the Government for making fish catch by Indian 
fishing vessels in the Exclusive Economic Zones and on the High Seas duty-free. This 
will encourage fishermen to explore beyond traditional waters and expand marine 
productivity. Under the PM Matsya Sampada Yojana, projects worth over Rs. 2,400/- 
crore have been sanctioned in Andhra Pradesh with substantial Central support.  
While implementation is underway, I urge a faster execution, so that infrastructure, 
cold chain and processing facilities translate into higher farmer incomes. I also 
114
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

request enhanced access to modern deep-sea fishing vessels and integrated supply 
chain facilities.  
Sir, as regards skill development and technology, this is the year of futuristic 
technology in India. From hosting the AI summit to promoting adoption of AI and 
blockchain, our country is leading technological change. Under the leadership of Sri 
Nara Chandrababu Naidu Garu and hon. Prime Minister Narendra Modi ji, Andhra 
Pradesh is once again emerging as a technology hub. Our State has captured over 25 
per cent of proposed investments in the first nine months of financial year 2026 and is 
a front-runner in attracting large-scale capital. Major hyper scale data centre 
proposals in Vishakhapatnam, close to US $ 25 billion investments have transformed 
the city into a major capital infrastructure hub. A Quantum Valley is being developed in 
Amaravati, Andhra Pradesh's capital, to boost India's global standing in next 
generation computing. Renewable energy sites and green hydrogen plants are being 
established to reduce carbon emissions and attract sustainable investments. 
However, specialized training is required. 
 Then, I come to Defence and strategic manufacturing. India's Defence 
production has surpassed Rs.1.5 lakh crores. Defence exports have crossed Rs. 
23,000/- crores. Andhra Pradesh, with its ports and industrial corridors, is well 
placed to host ancillary Defence manufacturing and logistics infrastructure. 
Sir, lastly, Andhra Pradesh has rich Buddhist heritage sites such as Amaravati, 
Addurru and Nagarjunakonda. Structured investment in Buddhist tourism 
infrastructure will generate employment and strengthen cultural engagement. In 
conclusion, Mr. Vice-Chairman, Sir, this Budget combines macroeconomic stability, 
infrastructure-led growth, technological transformation and social inclusion. With 
cooperative federalism and focussed implementation, Andhra Pradesh can accelerate 
its journey towards Swarna Andhra Pradesh while contributing to the national Vision of 
Viksit Bharat. I thank the hon. Finance Minister for having given us a good Budget and 
good allocation to Andhra Pradesh, particularly. I extend my full support to the Union 
Budget 2026-27. Jai Andhra, Jai Viksit Bharat. Thank you, Sir. 
THE VICE-CHAIRMAN (DR. DINESH SHARMA) : Next is, Dr. Kavita Patidar; not 
present. Dr. Fauzia Khan; four minutes.  
DR. FAUZIA KHAN (Maharashtra) : Sir, we stand today at decisive crossroads. 
India's much-debated demographic dividend rests not in abstract numbers, but in the 
165 million children below the age of six, children who will constitute our future 
[ 10 February,2026, ]
115

workforce. It is the bedrock of India's human capital and non-negotiable for a long-
term economic growth. I would like to ask the hon. Finance Minister – she is not here 
but I would like to ask her -- whether we can really build a Viksit Bharat without first 
securing the rights of our youngest non-voting citizens. ECD must be seen as a 
fundamental human right because over 85 per cent of brain development happens 
before the age of six. If we miss this critical window, no later intervention can fully 
compensate for whatever is lost. What we failed to build in the early years, we spend 
a lifetime trying to repair it. It is precisely this neglect of our earliest foundations that is 
now reflected in our weak human development outcomes.  
Sir, our poor Human Development Index stands well, well below the world 
average. Progress cannot be measured only through infrastructure or by doubling the 
number of schemes. We must look honestly at what our HDI tells us. The WCD 
Budget for 2026-27 is projected at Rs. 28,000 crores, yet child-focused expenditure, 
as a share of the Union Budget, has fallen by 1 per cent from 2017-18, while the total 
spending on children remains below 1 per cent of GDP, far short of the 5 per cent 
recommendation by the National Plan of Action for Children. Despite the NEP-2020, 
40 per cent of rural children aged three to six remain outside preschools or 
anganwadis. 
 Sir, Japan has produced a disciplined and innovative workforce because 
Japan's universal access to kindergartens and nurseries has 90 per cent attendance, 
strong subsidies and zero waiting lists. Sir, Singapore has a similar focus. We are 
held back by weak utilization and fragmentation. We do not lack resources. We have 
Rs. 3.5 lakh crores in cess funds lying dormant, including 45 per cent of construction 
cess unspent. Sir, 30 million children require protection, yet the funding remains 
skewed and siloed. We cannot limp. We cannot afford to limp. We will miss the bus. 
Sir, the vision of India in 2047 risks becoming an India strong on paper, hollow 
at the core. I urge three reforms. First, we need to establish dedicated child 
budgeting units across Ministries to end silos and enable integrated-planning. 
Second, we need to move to outcome-based budgeting. Without age and gender-
disaggregated data, today, we are flying blind. Third, there should be a national pre-
primary education policy; a national board for pre-primary education, investing in the 
appointment of specially-trained staff in anganwadis with expertise in foundational 
learning. We have to invest in this because a child born at this very moment will be a 
21-year-old citizen in 2047.

[ RAJYA SABHA ]
