SHRI NARHARI AMIN: &Hon. Vice Chairman Sir, this Budget for the year 2026-27 is a 
budget that fulfills the aspirations of 140 crore countrymen. It strengthens the 
country's economy and presents a roadmap for a developed India. It fulfills the 
dreams of the people and further strengthens the foundation of India's bright future. 
India is rapidly moving towards becoming the world's third-largest economy. The 
Budget further strengthens India's global role. The people of the country have 
resolved to see India as a developed nation, Viksit Bharat, by 2047. This Budget is 
historic and will further strengthen women's empowerment.  
Sir, the Finance Minister, Smt. Nirmala Sitharaman has created a new record 
by presenting the budget for the ninth time. Hon. Prime Minister, Shri Narendra Bhai 
 
&English translation of the original speech delivered in Gujarati. 
 
[ 10 February,2026, ]
129

Modi, has welcomed the Budget presented by the Finance Minister in Parliament. 
This Budget will further strengthen and accelerate the reform express on which India 
is riding. This Budget will give new energy and momentum to the economy. In this 
2026 Budget, the Finance Minister has announced the formation of a new high-level 
committee. This Committee will study the impact of new technologies like AI on 
India's service sector. The Government's aim is to empower people by providing 
them with education, jobs, and opportunities to become entrepreneurs. The Finance 
Minister stated in the Budget that this is the era of technology. Adopting new 
technologies will provide new opportunities for farmers, women, youth, and people 
with disabilities. This will enable people to learn new skills and increase job 
opportunities. Three AIIMS, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, specializing in 
Ayurveda will be opened in India, one of which will be in Jamnagar, Gujarat.  ₹10,000 
crore will be invested in the biopharma sector over the next five years. Seventeen 
medicines for diseases like cancer and diabetes will become cheaper, and these 
medicines will be manufactured in India.  Seven high-speed rail corridors and 20 new 
waterways will also be built in the country.  Interest received on vehicle accident 
claims will now be tax-free.  Any interest awarded to a person by the Motor Accident 
Claims Tribunal will be tax-free. A textile mega park will be established. The 
Government will start girls' hostels in every district. Relief will be provided for traveling 
abroad, studying abroad, and sending money abroad.   
Sir, the defense budget has been increased by 15%. The country's health 
budget has been increased to ₹104,599 crore.  ₹2.19 lakh crore has been allocated for 
capital expenditure. ₹63,733 crore will be spent on aircraft and aero-engines.  
₹25,023 crore has been allocated for the Navy. The electronics budget has been 
increased from ₹22,919 crore to ₹40,000 crore.  Emphasis has been placed on the 
production of mobile phones and electronic items. Indigenous products will be 
promoted. More investment will be made in textiles, Khadi, handlooms, handicrafts, 
and sports equipment.₹10,000 crore will be invested in biopharma, with 50% of the 
investment in Gujarat. 10 to 12 cluster plans will be developed in Gujarat. ₹1.30 lakh 
crore will be invested in semiconductor chips. The highest number of semiconductor 
chip plants, that is, five plants will be in Gujarat. ₹96,000 crore has been allocated for 
the VB G Ram G Yojana and ₹30,000 crore for the old MNREGA scheme. 
Employment will be provided for 125 days instead of 100 days, plus 60 days during the 
farming season, totaling 185 days of employment. 21 percent of the budget has been 
allocated for the development of rural areas and villages. Assistance to Panchayats 
will be doubled. The Government is moving towards self-reliant villages. A National

Drive Scheme will be prepared for farmers. Following Operation Sindoor, the defense 
budget has been increased by ₹1 lakh crore. Emphasis will be placed on the

percent, a rise of ₹2.19 crore. Borders with four countries will be monitored using 
drone cameras. Five medical hubs will be established with the participation of the 
private sector. New employment and job opportunities will be created in the 
pharmaceutical sector. New textile parks will be established. A package of ₹12,000 
crore has been announced for this. TCS on remittances sent abroad has been 
reduced from five percent to two percent. Rupees 12.2 lakh crore has been allocated

been allocated for security along the Gujarat-Pakistan water and land border.1 3,537 
Pradhan Mantri Jan Aushadhi Kendras have been launched across the country, 
providing medicines at 80% subsidized rates. This has saved people approximately 
₹38,000 crore.  Under the Pradhan Mantri Garib Kalyan Anna Yojana, 813.5 million 
poor people are provided 5 kg of grain free of cost. Under the Pradhan Mantri Jan 
Dhan Yojana, bank accounts of 567.2 million beneficiaries have been opened with ₹0.

LPG gas connections. Under the Pradhan Mantri Swanidhi Yojana, loans worth 
₹13,736.14 crore have been sanctioned to 6.8 million street vendors, of which 65% of 
the beneficiaries are from the SC, ST, and OBC categories. More than 41.91 crore 
Ayushman cards have been issued under the Pradhan Mantri Jan Arogya Yojana 
(Ayushman Bharat). More than 9.42 crore beneficiaries have received free treatment. 
The Ayushman Bharat scheme provides free treatment up to ₹5 lakh. In Gujarat, this 
scheme and the “Maa Amritam Yojana ” together provide free treatment up to ₹10 
lakh. Over 4 crore pucca houses have been built for the poor in the last 10 years.

drinking water in the last 5 years.  Under the Pradhan Mantri Kisan Samman Nidhi, ₹4 
lakh crore has been deposited in farmers' accounts.  Prioritizing quality education, 
over 400 Eklavya Model Residential Schools have been started in tribal areas.Under 
the Pradhan Mantri Gram Sadak Yojana, 18,000 km of new roads have been built, 
connecting rural India. 150 Vande Bharat trains running from Jammu to Kerala 
demonstrate the Government's commitment to high-speed transportation. Under the 
visionary leadership of hon. Prime Minister Shri Narendra Modi, the metro network 
has crossed the 1,000 km mark, making India the third largest metro network in the

world. In 2014, the number of income tax return filers was 36 million. Today, in 2025-

THE VICE-CHAIRMAN (SHRI BHUBANESWAR KALITA) : Hon. Members, the 
discussion on General Budget, 2026-27 will continue tomorrow. Now, we will take up 
the permitted list of Special Mentions.  The first Special Mention is of hon. Member, 
Shri Lahar Singh Siroya, on ‘Demand for protecting cultural livelihoods by integrating 
'Crafts Villages.’
 
SPECIAL MENTIONS 
Demand for protecting cultural livelihoods by integrating 'Crafts Villages’ 
SHRI LAHAR SINGH SIROYA (Karnataka): Sir, I rise to submit matter concerning the 
need to integrate cultural preservation with highway infrastructure development, 
especially in Karnataka. The Bengaluru-Mysuru Expressway, a flagship project of the 
Central Government, has impacted thousands of artisans and small business owners 
in bypassed towns.  Iconic, globally recognized traditions like the G.I. Tag, 
Channapatna wooden toys, and regional culinary heritage are seeing business drop 
by as much as 70-80 per cent. As I have urged previously, the construction of world-
class roads must not unintentionally destroy the vibrant economic and cultural fabrics 
of our smaller towns. I had raised this issue earlier as a special mention on December 
19, 2022. However, the situation of our artisans remains largely unchanged. 
132
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

I, therefore, make an earnest request to the Union Minister for Road Transport 
and Highways and MSME to immediately implement the crafts village, i.e., the 
Shilpgrama type of concept, along the new corridors starting with the Bangalore-
Mysore Expressway. This requires two essential steps. 
One, mandate the identification and development of permanent high-visibility 
sites or suitable Government land adjacent to the new expressways to serve as a 
marketplace for local artisans and MSMEs. Two, provide a dedicated central 
mechanism for financial and infrastructure support to develop these villages into 
state-of-the-art hubs that safeguard the livelihood of our traditional craftsmen and 
advance our Prime Minister's Vocal for Local vision. 
We must ensure modern connectivity also secures the future of our unique 
cultural heritage. I urge the Government to look into it. 
THE VICE-CHAIRMAN (SHRI BHUBANESWAR KALITA) : The following hon. 
Members associated themselves with the Special Mention raised by hon. Member, 
Shri Lahar Singh Siroya:  Dr. Sasmit Patra (Odisha), Shri R. Girirajan (Tamil Nadu), 
Shri A.A. Rahim (Kerala), Dr. Fauzia Khan (Maharashtra), Dr. Bhim Singh (Bihar), 
Shri Sadanand Mhalu Shet Tanavade (Goa) and Shrimati Sumitra Balmik (Madhya 
Pradesh).  
 Now, Shri I.S. Inbadurai. Your subject is: Agniveers’ Transition to Civilian Life: 
A Responsible and Forward-Looking Approach. 
Need for a responsible and forward-looking approach for Agniveers’ transition to 
civilian life 
SHRI I.S. INBADURAI (Tamil Nadu) : Mr. Vice-Chairman, Sir, I rise to address the 
Government on the future of our Agniveers, particularly the first batch recruited under 
the Agnipath scheme, of which around 40,000 young Agniveers are expected to 
complete their four-year tenure and exit the Armed Forces in 2026. These young 
citizens joined the forces at a very early age and served the nation with dedication, 
discipline, and pride. The Government fully recognizes its responsibility towards 
them, not just during service, but equally after their exit. 
As per the scheme, up to 25 per cent of each batch will be given the 
opportunity for permanent enrolment in the Armed Forces based on merit and 
performance. The remaining 75 per cent will transition to civilian life with a tax-free 
[ 10 February,2026, ]
133

Seva Nidhi package of about Rs. 11.71 lakh, providing them financial security at a 
crucial stage. 
The Government has taken comprehensive steps for their re-employment and 
reintegration. There is 10 percent reservation for ex-Agniveers in CAPF and the 
Ministry of Defence, along with age relaxation. Many states have extended similar 
benefits in police and public sector jobs. 
I urge the Government to ensure skill certificates, educational support through 
NIOS and IGNOU, access to credit for entrepreneurship, and a dedicated placement 
portal through inter-ministerial coordination for Agniveers, so as to ensure that their 
service to the nation becomes a strong foundation for a secure and dignified future.  
Thank you, Sir. 
THE VICE-CHAIRMAN (SHRI BHUBANESWAR KALITA) : The following hon. 
Members associated themselves with the Special Mention raised by hon. Member, 
Shri I.S. Inbadurai: Dr. Sasmit Patra (Odisha), Dr. Fauzia Khan (Maharashtra) and 
Dr. M. Dhanapal (Tamil Nadu).  
 Next, Dr. Sasmit Patra.   
Concern over the non-functioning of the Press Council of India 
DR. SASMIT PATRA (Odisha) : Mr. Vice-Chairman, Sir, there is concern over the 
prolonged non-functioning of the Press Council of India, a statutory and autonomous 
body established by an Act of Parliament in 1978 to safeguard the freedom of the 
press and uphold ethical standards in journalism. The 14th Press Council completed 
its term on October 5, 2024, yet the 15th Press Council has not been convened even 
after more than sixteen months. The 14 th Press Council completed its term on 5 th 
October 2024, yet the 15 th Press Council has not been convened even after more than 
16 months. The situation has been further aggravated by the expiry of the extended 
tenure of the former Chairperson, Justice Ranjana Desai, on 17 th December 2025, 
without the appointment of a successor. The absence of a duly constituted Council 
and a Chairperson has serious implications.  I urge the Government to treat this 
matter with the seriousness it deserves and to take immediate steps to complete the 
appointment of the Chairperson and constitute the 15 th Press Council without any 
further delay. Restoring the full functioning of the Press Council of India is essential to 
uphold democratic values, strengthen media accountability and reaffirm the 
Government’s commitment to a free, fair and responsible Press. I thank you, Sir.                                               
134
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

 The next speaker is Shri A.A. Rahim. 
Need to allow flexibility in utilisation of Tide Over Rice Allocations to Kerala and 
release pending NFSA dues 
SHRI A.A. RAHIM (Kerala): Sir, I thank you for giving me this opportunity. Allotment 
of food grains from the Government of India is a major source for Kerala to meet 
domestic requirements. The Government of Kerala receives 33,294.198 metric tonnes 
of Tide Over rice every month, which is promptly distributed among beneficiaries who 
fall outside the coverage of the National Food Security Act. However, due to the 
extraordinary circumstances arising from the floods and the COVID-19 pandemic, the 
State was unable to distribute 99,213 metric tonnes of the allotted rice. This difficulty 
could have been avoided had the State been permitted to adjust its distribution on a 
quarterly or annual basis, allowing for better management during unforeseen crises. 
At a time when market prices of rice are rising steadily, reallocating this rice to Tide 
Over beneficiaries would provide significant relief to many families. Such a move 
136
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

would also help moderate prices in the open market and serve as an effective market-
intervention measure. 
Therefore, I request the Union Government to kindly grant permission for the 
utilisation of rice above the present Tide Over limit for distribution, and to allow the 
State the flexibility to adjust its quarterly allocations to prevent future accumulation of 
stocks. I also urge the Union Government to release the pending amount of 
Rs. 22,12,83,743 under the “Assistance to State Agencies for Intra-State Movement 
of Foodgrains and FPS Dealers’ Margin“ component of the NFSA, 2013, for the period 
from 2018 to 2023. Thank you, Sir.

Need for redressal of development grievances of Eastern Nagaland in the wake of 
creation of Frontier Nagaland Territorial Authority 
SHRIMATI S. PHANGNON KONYAK (Nagaland) : The subject of my Special Mention 
is, “Need for Redressal of Development Grievances of Eastern Nagaland in wake of  
creation of Frontier Nagaland Territorial Authority. ” The Eastern Nagaland Region 
(undivided Tuensang District) comprises six districts, namely, Tuensang, Mon, 
Longleng, Kiphire, Noklak and Shamator. The Eastern Nagaland Peoples’ 
Organisation (ENPO), an apex organisation representing the eight recognised Naga 
tribes of Chang, Sumi (Eastern) Khaimnungan, Konyak, Phom, Sangtam, Tikhir, 
Yimkhiung, has been demanding for about two decades for a separate, full-fledged 
State on various grounds including historical background and socio-economic 
backwardness of the region. 
On behalf of lakhs of people of the Frontier Nagaland Territory in the interior 
villages and peripheries, I take this opportunity to thank the hon. Prime Minister for his 
vision of Peace, Progress and Prosperity which is translating on ground and to the 
hon. Union Home Minister for his decisive leadership which has paved the way for the 
historic signing of the creation of Frontier Nagaland Territorial Authority on 5.02.2026. 
This historic signing of the Agreement is a testament of fulfilment of grassroots 
[ 10 February,2026, ]
137

aspirations as this was a mass people’s movement from the peripheries. I once again, 
on behalf of the people of Frontier Nagaland Territory, thank the Government for this 
great step and urge for early redressal of long-standing economic and equitable 
development grievances of Eastern Nagaland. Thank you, Sir.

Concern over Quantum Valley and Quantum Teaching Labs 
SHRI MASTHAN RAO YADAV BEEDHA (Andhra Pradesh ): Sir, the subject of my 
Special Mention is, ‘Concern over Quantum Valley and Quantum Teaching Labs.’ I 
draw attention of the Government to the need for supporting Andhra Pradesh’s 
Quantum Valley initiative and for including the State in the national programme for 
establishing Quantum Teaching Labs in engineering institutions. Quantum technology 
is an emerging and strategic field with applications in computing, communications, 
cyber security, healthcare, materials science and defence. Andhra Pradesh has taken 
proactive steps through the proposed Quantum Valley initiative to build an ecosystem 
for advanced research, education and industry collaboration. However, the success 
of this initiative depends on strong support from the Union Government and its 
integration with national efforts such as the National Quantum Mission. Establishing 
Quantum Teaching Labs in Engineering colleges across the State will help create a 
skilled workforce by providing students with hands-on training and early exposure to 
quantum concepts. These labs will strengthen research capacity, improve faculty 
skills and bridge the gap between academic learning and advanced technologies. 
Inclusion of Andhra Pradesh in the national rollout will also ensure regional balance 
and equal access to modern scientific infrastructure.  
I request the Union Government to extend financial and institutional support to 
the Quantum Valley initiative and include Andhra Pradesh under a national programme 
for setting up Quantum Teaching Labs, so as to promote innovation, generate high-
skilled employment and strengthen India’s leadership and self-reliance in emerging 
technologies. 
138
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

Khan (Maharashtra).  
Demand for direct international flights from Tirupati to Dubai and Abu Dhabi 
SHRI MEDA RAGHUNADHA REDDY (Andhra Pradesh): Sir, I draw attention of the 
Government to the need for commencing direct international flight services from 
Tirupati Airport to Dubai and Abu Dhabi, which are major global aviation hubs 
providing seamless onward connectivity to Europe and the United States of America. 
A substantial number of residents from Annamayya, YSR Kadapa, Nellore, 
Ananthapur and Chittoor districts of Andhra Pradesh are employed in Gulf, Europe 
and Western countries.  Many passengers from this region travel frequently for 
employment, higher education, business and family purposes.  Due to the absence of 
direct international connectivity from Tirupati, passengers have to travel to Chennai, 
Bengaluru or Hyderabad to board international flights.  This causes avoidable financial 
burden, longer transit time and physical hardship, particularly for senior citizens, 
women, and children.  
Sir, Tirupati is one of the most important spiritual destinations in the country, 
being the gateway to Tirumala. Direct international flights from Tirupati would greatly 
facilitate NRIs residing in Gulf and Western countries to directly visit Tirumala for 
darshan without multiple domestic transits. Considering the large expatriate 
population from the Rayalaseema region, growing international passenger demand 
and strategic importance of Dubai and Abu Dhabi as global transit hubs, there is a 
strong and compelling case for initiating direct international flights from Tirupati.  
I, therefore, urge the Government to initiate necessary steps in coordination 
with airlines and airport authorities to operationalize direct international flight services 
from Tirupati to Dubai and Abu Dhabi at the earliest in the interest of passengers and 
regional development.  
THE VICE-CHAIRMAN (SHRI BHUBANESWAR KALITA): The following hon. 
Members associated themselves with the Special Mention made by hon. Member,

(Kerala), Shri Niranjan Bishi (Odisha), Shrimati Sulata Deo (Odisha), Shri Subhasish 
Khuntia (Odisha), Shri Golla Baburao (Andhra Pradesh), Shri Subhas Chandra Bose
